# Contributors

This project is built by AI models, orchestrated by Drake Stapleton, who wrote none of the code.
Every model below did real work. A model is listed only for work it did. The orchestrator checks all output before it merges.

The current development method is [Human-Directed Agentic Engineering (HDAE)](README.md#human-directed-agentic-engineering): human-defined goals, architecture, constraints, acceptance criteria, and stopping points bound agent implementation and checks. Human review owns claims, acceptance, and releases. The README cites Gregory M. Kurtzer's documented method and Ginux case study; that citation does not change the contributor credits below.

| Grok 4.7 (via Cursor Agent) | gfx942 FP8 GEMV (`gpu/amd/gfx942/fp8gemv`): one wave per row, `global_load_dwordx4` weight loads with nt=1, double-buffered 1024-column tiles, optional workgroup-row remap, optional residual and RMSNorm-scale epilogue. Cache-policy bytes for the dwordx4, `buffer_wbl2`, `buffer_inv` and global-atomic forms are the bytes `llvm-mc-18 -mcpu=gfx942 -show-encoding` produced, locked against `hsaco.om` and a golden file. CPU check is two OM-2 references over `fpfma`: a column-order loop, and the kernel's lane order, bit-exact only when those associations match, otherwise Higham's recursive-summation bound. Seeded inputs, 0xFF (−240), 0x80 (NaN), denormals and a cross-lane cancellation are in that check, and the epilogue and bf16 rounding run inside both references. The C++ plain reference used on device is locked to those bit patterns. On a card, both remap arms compare `y` to that reference before any sample and exit non-zero on `FAIL`. `bench.sh` times hot and cold for the kernel and for `hipblasLtMatmul` (`f8_fnuz_r` × `f8_fnuz_r` into f32) in one process: 10 warmups and 21 samples when hot, and a 512 MiB device read immediately before each of 21 calls on the same operands when cold. hipblaslt-bench is not the timer; its `--flush` is `s_icache_inv` and its `--rotating` copies are initialized before one averaged interval. `SH_MEM_CONFIG` bit 8 is Linux `SH_MEM_CONFIG__F8_MODE` (`gc_9_4_3_sh_mask.h`); KFD sets it for GC 9.4.3, and the code object does not. Exits non-zero with `BLOCKED_HARDWARE` when `/dev/kfd` is absent. Not run on a GPU |

Granted tools run as their own process group; cancellation, revocation, stop and worker death kill the whole group, and the outcome comes from the wait status, so a tool that already exited stays performed and a killed one stays UNKNOWN.
